---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n367\t# Internal state + classification helpers\n368\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n369\t\n370\t\n371\t@dataclass\n372\tclass _LifecycleState:\n373\t    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n374\t\n375\t    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n376\t    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n377\t    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n378\t    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n379\t    \"\"\"\n380\t\n381\t    spec: EvalSpec\n382\t    observed: ExpectResult) -> None:\n391\t        self.expects.append(result)\n392\t\n393\t    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n394\t        self.harness_failure_step = step\n395\t        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n396\t\n397\t\n398\tdef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n399\t    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n400\t\n401\t    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n402\t\n403\t    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n404\t      fully-qualified classname.\n405\t    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n406\t    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n407\t\n408\t    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n409\t    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n410\t    \"\"\"\n411\t\n412\t    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n413\t        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n414\t        cls = type(exc)\n415\t        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n416\t\n417\t    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n418\t        return \"PASS\", None\n419\t\n420\t    return \"FAIL\", None\n421\t\n422\t\n423\tdef _finalize(\n424\t    *,\n425\t    state: _LifecycleState,\n426\t    home: HomeIsolation,\n427\t    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n428\t    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n429\t) -> EvalOutcome:\n430\t    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n431\t\n432\t    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n433\t    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n434\t Teardown failures are routed through\n436\t    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n437\t    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n438\t    \"\"\"\n439\t\n440\t    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n441\t\n442\t    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n443\t    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n444\t\n445\t    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n446\t    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n447\t    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n448\t    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n449\t    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n450\t        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n451\t        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n452\t        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n453\t        duration_sec = 0.0\n454\t\n455\t    return EvalOutcome(\n456\t        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n457\t        title=state.spec.title,\n458\t        status=status,\n459\t        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n460\t        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n461\t        skip_reason=None,\n462\t        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n463\t        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n464\t        error_class=error_class,\n465\t    )\n466\t\n467\t\n468\tdef _safe_teardown(\n469\t    home: HomeIsolation,\n470\t    *,\n471\t    keep: bool,\n472\t    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n473\t) -> None:\n474\t    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n475\t\n476\t    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n477\t    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n478\t    docstring). Field shape matches the JSONL contract documented\n507\t    in ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md``.\n508\t    \"\"\"\n509\t\n510\t    event: str\n511\t    ts_offset_sec: float\n512\t    eval_id: str\n513\t    step: str\n514\t    extra: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n515\t\n516\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n517\t        # Build an ordered dict so JSON diff output stays stable; the\n518\t        # round() pin is documented in D-0049/spec.md (\"Rounded to\n519\t        # microseconds so JSON diff output stays compact and stable.\").\n520\t        return {\n521\t            \"event\": self.event,\n522\t            \"ts_offset_sec\": round(self.ts_offset_sec, 6),\n523\t            \"eval_id\": self.eval_id,\n524\t            \"step\": self.step,\n525\t            \"extra\": dict(self.extra),\n526\t        }\n527\t\n528\t\n529\tclass _JsonlLog:\n530\t    \"\"\"Thread-safe buffer + writer for the per-eval JSONL log.\n531\t\n532\t    The runner emits events from both the worker thread (every\n533\t    lifecycle step except timeout teardown) and the main thread (the\n534\t    ``timeout_fired`` + ``outcome`` events). run(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n779\t        \"\"\"Execute the per-eval lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n780\t\n781\t        Wraps :func:`run_eval` with logging proxies + a per-eval timeout\n782\t        budget. The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n783\t        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n784\t        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n785\t        branch this class adds.\n786\t\n787\t        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n788\t        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n789\t        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n790\t        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n791\t        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n792\t        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n793\t        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n794\t        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n795\t        \"\"\"\n796\t\n797\t        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n798\t        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n799\t\n800\t        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. The proxy already logs the failure via\n834\t        # ``EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR``; the callback is a no-op so the\n835\t        # caller-injected behaviour (preserved by FR-LC1) keeps working\n836\t        # if a future caller wires one through ``EvalRunner``.\n837\t        def on_teardown_error(_exc: BaseException) -> None:\n838\t            return None\n839\t\n840\t        def worker() -> None:\n841\t            try:\n842\t                outcome_holder[\"result\"] = run_eval(\n843\t                    spec,\n844\t                    home=logged_home,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n852\t                    expect_callables=wrapped_expects,\n853\t                    deploy_hooks=logged_deploy,\n854\t                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n855\t                    keep_home_on_pass=self._keep_home_on_pass,\n856\t                )\n857\t            except BaseException as exc:  # makes a hung worker rare in production.\n863\t        thread = threading.Thread(\n864\t            target=worker,\n865\t            name=f\"EvalRunner[{spec.id}]\",\n866\t            daemon=True,\n867\t        )\n868\t        thread.start()\n869\t        thread.join(timeout=timeout_sec)\n870\t\n871\t        if thread.is_alive():\n872\t            return self._handle_timeout(spec=spec, log=log, timeout_sec=timeout_sec)\n873\t\n874\t        # Either run_eval produced an outcome or raised\n875\t        # an interrupt; the latter must propagate to the orchestrator so\n876\t        # NFR-REL1 can mark INTERRUPTED. Flush the JSONL log first so\n877\t        # the partial events survive the re-raise.\n878\t        if \"exc\" in exc_holder:\n879\t            exc = exc_holder[\"exc\"]\n880\t            # NFR-REL1 (T03.07): when a cancellation token is wired, the\n881\t            # runner converts a worker-thread KeyboardInterrupt /\n882\t            # SystemExit directly into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome so the\n883\t            # orchestrator's ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed loop never\n884\t            # has to translate an exception into a status. Without a\n885\t            # token, propagation is preserved verbatim so the FR-LC1\n886\t            # contract (and the existing ``test_runner_class.py`` AC4\n887\t            # case) stays unchanged.\n888\t            if (\n889\t                self._cancellation_token is not None\n890\t                and isinstance(exc, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))\n891\t            ):\n892\t                self._cancellation_token.cancel()\n893\t                return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n894\t            self._flush_log(log)\n895\t            raise exc\n896\t\n897\t        outcome = outcome_holder[\"result\"]\n898\t        # Record the terminal classification so post-mortem tooling can\n899\t        # tell whether the run ended cleanly or was reclassified.\n900\t        log.emit(\n901\t            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n902\t            step=\"outcome\",\n903\t            extra={\n904\t                \"status\": outcome.status,\n905\t                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n906\t                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n907\t            },\n908\t        )\n909\t        self._flush_log(log)\n910\t        return outcome\n911\t\n912\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n913\t    # Internals\n914\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n915\t\n916\t    def _resolve_timeout(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> Optional[float]:\n917\t        \"\"\"Return the resolved per-eval timeout, or ``None`` for unbounded.\n918\t\n919\t        ``spec.timeout_sec`` takes precedence; if absent or 0 the\n920\t        runner falls back to ``default_timeout_sec``. ``0`` is treated\n921\t        as \"use the default\" rather than \"no timeout\" because a literal\n922\t        zero would always fire and is never useful in practice.\n923\t        \"\"\"\n924\t        spec_timeout = spec.timeout_sec\n925\t        if spec_timeout is not None and spec_timeout > 0:\n926\t            return float(spec_timeout)\n927\t        if self._default_timeout_sec is not None and self._default_timeout_sec > 0:\n928\t            return float(self._default_timeout_sec)\n929\t        return None\n930\t\n931\t    def _handle_timeout(\n932\t        self,\n933\t        *,\n934\t        spec: EvalSpec,\n935\t        log: _JsonlLog,\n936\t        timeout_sec: Optional[float],\n937\t    ) -> EvalOutcome:\n938\t        \"\"\"Emit timeout events, attempt best-effort teardown, return outcome.\n939\t\n940\t        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) extends the T03.05 timeout path with a\n941\t        subprocess kill + zombie reap step: if the wired executor\n942\t        exposes a ``cancel()`` method (the production\n943\t        ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` + ``PtyDriver`` does), the runner calls\n944\t        it from the main thread so the PtyDriver child terminates and\n945\t        ``wait()`` reaps the zombie before the orchestrator collects\n946\t        the next outcome. Failure is swallowed because the TIMEOUT\n947\t        outcome must always return cleanly; the JSONL log records the\n948\t        cancel attempt.\n949\t        \"\"\"\n950\t        budget = float(timeout_sec) if timeout_sec is not None else 0.0\n951\t        log.emit(\n952\t            EvalRunner.EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED,\n953\t            step=\"timeout\",\n954\t            extra={\"timeout_sec\": budget},\n955\t        )\n956\t\n957\t        # NFR-REL1: kill the executor's subprocess + reap zombie before\n958\t        # we attempt teardown. Documented on D-0050/spec.md.\n959\t        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"timeout\")\n960\t\n961\t        log.emit(\n962\t            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n963\t            step=\"teardown\",\n964\t            extra={\"keep\": True},\n965\t        )\n966\t        try:\n967\t            # ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec: EvalSpec\n    observed: ObservedRun = _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The ``keep`` Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n        duration_sec = 0.0\n\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n        title=state.spec.title,\n        status=status,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n        skip_reason=None,\n        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n        error_class=error_class,\n    )\n\n\ndef _safe_teardown(\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    *,\n    keep: bool,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n\n    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n    docstring). The optional callback lets COMP-004 (T03.05) record\n    teardown failures into its per-eval JSONL log without re-raising\n    from the lifecycle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        home.teardown(keep=keep)\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        raise\n    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — swallowed per spec\n        if on_teardown_error is not None:\n            try:\n                on_teardown_error(exc)\n            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 — callback failure is non-fatal\n                pass\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# COMP-004 EvalRunner class (T03.05 / D-0049)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LogEvent:\n    \"\"\"Single JSONL line buffered by :class:`EvalRunner`.\n\n    The buffer holds events until ``home_path`` is known; once setup\n    completes the buffer is flushed to disk and subsequent events are\n    appended directly. Field shape matches the JSONL contract documented\n    in ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    event: str\n    ts_offset_sec: float\n    eval_id: str\n    step: str\n    extra: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        # Build an ordered dict so JSON diff output stays stable; the\n        # round() pin is documented in D-0049/spec.md (\"Rounded to\n        # microseconds so JSON diff output stays compact and stable.\").\n        return {\n            \"event\": self.event,\n            \"ts_offset_sec\": round(self.ts_offset_sec, 6),\n            \"eval_id\": self.eval_id,\n            \"step\": self.step,\n            \"extra\": dict(self.extra),\n        }\n\n\nclass _JsonlLog:\n    \"\"\"Thread-safe buffer + writer for the per-eval JSONL log.\n\n    The runner emits events from both the worker thread (every\n    lifecycle step except timeout teardown) and the main thread (the\n    ``timeout_fired`` + ``outcome`` events). A lock keeps the ordered\n    list of buffered events consistent and `write()` deterministic.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *, eval_id: str, clock: Callable[[], float]) -> None:\n        self._eval_id = eval_id\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._start = clock()\n        self._lock = threading.Lock()\n        self._events: list[_LogEvent] = []\n\n    def _ts_offset(self) -> float:\n        return self._clock() - self._start\n\n    def emit(self, event: str, *, step: str, extra: Optional[Mapping[str, Any]] = None) -> None:\n        evt = _LogEvent(\n            event=event,\n            ts_offset_sec=self._ts_offset(),\n            eval_id=self._eval_id,\n            step=step,\n            extra=dict(extra or {}),\n        )\n        with self._lock:\n            self._events.append(evt)\n\n    def snapshot(self) -> tuple[_LogEvent, ...]:\n        with self._lock:\n            return tuple(self._events)\n\n    def write_to(self, path: Path) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the buffered events as one JSON object per line.\n\n        Failures are best-effort: the JSONL log is a forensic artifact,\n        not a load-bearing part of the outcome contract.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lock:\n            events = list(self._events)\n        try:\n            path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            with path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n                for evt in events:\n                    fh.write(json.dumps(evt.to_dict(), sort_keys=False))\n                    fh.write(\"\\n\")\n        except OSError:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort log write\n            return\n\n\nclass _LoggingHomeProxy:\n    \"\"\"Duck-typed proxy around ``HomeIsolation`` emitting setup/teardown events.\n\n    ``HomeIsolation`` is a frozen dataclass so we cannot subclass-and-\n    override its setup/teardown methods. Wrapping the instance keeps the\n    skeleton (``run_eval``) untouched while letting ``EvalRunner`` log\n    the four required events at the exact lifecycle boundary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, home: Any, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._home = home\n        self._log = log\n\n    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_STARTED, step=\"setup\")\n        try:\n            home_path = self._home.setup(config=config)\n        except BaseException:\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"setup\",\n            extra={\"home_path\": str(home_path)},\n        )\n        return home_path\n\n    def teardown(self, keep: bool) -> None:\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": keep},\n        )\n        try:\n            self._home.teardown(keep)\n        except BaseException as exc:\n            cls = type(exc)\n            self._log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED, step=\"teardown\")\n\n    def env(self) -> Mapping[str, str]:\n        return self._home.env()\n\n    @property\n    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n        return self._home.home_path\n\n\nclass _LoggingExecutor:\n    \"\"\"LifecycleExecutor proxy emitting spawn/inject/observe events.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, executor: LifecycleExecutor, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._log = log\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED, step=\"spawn\")\n        self._executor.spawn(ctx)\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_INJECT_STARTED, step=\"inject\")\n        self._executor.inject(ctx)\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> ObservedRun:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED, step=\"observe\")\n        observed = self._executor.observe(ctx)\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"observe\",\n            extra={\n                \"exit_code\": observed.exit_code,\n                \"duration_sec\": observed.duration_sec,\n            },\n        )\n        return observed\n\n\ndef _wrap_expect_with_log(\n    callable_: ExpectCallable, *, index: int, log: _JsonlLog\n) -> ExpectCallable:\n    \"\"\"Return a wrapped ExpectCallable that emits assertion events.\n\n    The wrapper preserves the original signature so ``run_eval`` cannot\n    tell it is being observed. The original callable's ``__name__`` is\n    captured into the ``assertion_started`` / ``assertion_completed``\n    event payloads so post-mortem tooling can correlate JSONL rows with\n    DSL primitives.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name = getattr(callable_, \"__name__\", None)\n    if name == \"<lambda>\":\n        name = None\n\n    def _logging_expect(ctx: EvalContext) -> ExpectResult:\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name},\n        )\n        result = callable_(ctx)\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name, \"passed\": bool(result.passed)},\n        )\n        return result\n\n    # Preserve attributes so frameworks that introspect __name__ still work.\n    _logging_expect.__wrapped__ = callable_  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n    if name is not None:\n        _logging_expect.__name__ = name\n    return _logging_expect\n\n\nclass EvalRunner:\n    \"\"\"COMP-004 per-eval runner with JSONL logging + timeout enforcement.\n\n    Wraps the FR-LC1 lifecycle skeleton (``run_eval``) in a class that\n    owns two cross-cutting concerns the skeleton intentionally deferred:\n\n    1. A per-eval JSONL log under\n       ``home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`` covering every\n       lifecycle transition.\n    2. Per-eval timeout enforcement — when\n       ``EvalSpec.timeout_sec`` is set, the runner returns an\n       ``EvalOutcome`` with status ``TIMEOUT`` if the lifecycle does not\n       complete within budget.\n\n    See ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md`` for the full contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    LOG_DIR_RELPATH: ClassVar[Path] = Path(\".eval-logs\")\n\n    EVENT_SETUP_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_started\"\n    EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_completed\"\n    EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED: ClassVar[str] = \"hooks_deployed\"\n    EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"spawn_started\"\n    EVENT_INJECT_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"inject_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_completed\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_started\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_started\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_error\"\n    EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"timeout_fired\"\n    EVENT_INTERRUPTED_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"interrupted_fired\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_started\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_completed\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_error\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_SKIPPED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_skipped\"\n    EVENT_OUTCOME: ClassVar[str] = \"outcome\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        home: HomeIsolation,\n        config: EvalConfig,\n        executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n        deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n        keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n        default_timeout_sec: Optional[float] = None,\n        clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._home = home\n        self._config = config\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._run_dir = run_dir\n        self._artifacts_dir = artifacts_dir\n        self._stdout_path = stdout_path\n        self._stderr_path = stderr_path\n        self._transcript_path = transcript_path\n        self._expect_callables = tuple(expect_callables)\n        self._deploy_hooks = deploy_hooks\n        self._keep_home_on_pass = keep_home_on_pass\n        self._default_timeout_sec = default_timeout_sec\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Execute the per-eval lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n        Wraps :func:`run_eval` with logging proxies + a per-eval timeout\n        budget. The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n        branch this class adds.\n\n        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n\n        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. If the signal handler has\n        # already flipped the token before we even start the worker,\n        # return INTERRUPTED immediately so the orchestrator does not\n        # waste a HomeIsolation setup on an eval whose result will be\n        # discarded anyway.\n        if self._cancellation_token is not None and self._cancellation_token.is_cancelled():\n            return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n\n        # Holders for the worker thread. ``outcome_holder`` is populated\n        # on clean return; ``exc_holder`` is populated when run_eval\n        # raised (KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit, mainly — every other\n        # exception is folded into an ERRORED outcome by run_eval\n        # itself).\n        outcome_holder: dict[str, EvalOutcome] = {}\n        exc_holder: dict[str, BaseException] = {}\n\n        logged_home = _LoggingHomeProxy(self._home, log)\n        logged_executor = _LoggingExecutor(self._executor, log)\n        wrapped_expects = tuple(\n            _wrap_expect_with_log(c, index=i, log=log)\n            for i, c in enumerate(self._expect_callables)\n        )\n\n        # The wrapped ``deploy_hooks`` emits ``hooks_deployed`` once the\n        # underlying call returns; the spec does not require this event\n        # but it is the natural place to record that step 2 completed.\n        original_deploy = self._deploy_hooks\n\n        def logged_deploy(home_path: Path) -> None:\n            original_deploy(home_path)\n            log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED, step=\"deploy_hooks\")\n\n        # ``on_teardown_error`` is invoked by ``_safe_teardown`` when\n        # teardown raises. The proxy already logs the failure via\n        # ``EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR``; the callback is a no-op so the\n        # caller-injected behaviour (preserved by FR-LC1) keeps working\n        # if a future caller wires one through ``EvalRunner``.\n        def on_teardown_error(_exc: BaseException) -> None:\n            return None\n\n        def worker() -> None:\n            try:\n                outcome_holder[\"result\"] = run_eval(\n                    spec,\n                    home=logged_home,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    config=self._config,\n                    run_dir=self._run_dir,\n                    artifacts_dir=self._artifacts_dir,\n                    stdout_path=self._stdout_path,\n                    stderr_path=self._stderr_path,\n                    transcript_path=self._transcript_path,\n                    executor=logged_executor,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    expect_callables=wrapped_expects,\n                    deploy_hooks=logged_deploy,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=self._keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for re-raise\n                exc_holder[\"exc\"] = exc\n\n        # ``daemon=True`` so a hung worker cannot block process exit.\n        # NFR-REL1 (T03.07) wires the PtyDriver kill + zombie reap that\n        # makes a hung worker rare in production.\n        thread = threading.Thread(\n            target=worker,\n            name=f\"EvalRunner[{spec.id}]\",\n            daemon=True,\n        )\n        thread.start()\n        thread.join(timeout=timeout_sec)\n\n        if thread.is_alive():\n            return self._handle_timeout(spec=spec, log=log, timeout_sec=timeout_sec)\n\n        # Worker returned. Either run_eval produced an outcome or raised\n        # an interrupt; the latter must propagate to the orchestrator so\n        # NFR-REL1 can mark INTERRUPTED. Flush the JSONL log first so\n        # the partial events survive the re-raise.\n        if \"exc\" in exc_holder:\n            exc = exc_holder[\"exc\"]\n            # NFR-REL1 (T03.07): when a cancellation token is wired, the\n            # runner converts a worker-thread KeyboardInterrupt /\n            # SystemExit directly into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome so the\n            # orchestrator's ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed loop never\n            # has to translate an exception into a status. Without a\n            # token, propagation is preserved verbatim so the FR-LC1\n            # contract (and the existing ``test_runner_class.py`` AC4\n            # case) stays unchanged.\n            if (\n                self._cancellation_token is not None\n                and isinstance(exc, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))\n            ):\n                self._cancellation_token.cancel()\n                return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n            self._flush_log(log)\n            raise exc\n\n        outcome = outcome_holder[\"result\"]\n        # Record the terminal classification so post-mortem tooling can\n        # tell whether the run ended cleanly or was reclassified.\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _resolve_timeout(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> Optional[float]:\n        \"\"\"Return the resolved per-eval timeout, or ``None`` for unbounded.\n\n        ``spec.timeout_sec`` takes precedence; if absent or 0 the\n        runner falls back to ``default_timeout_sec``. ``0`` is treated\n        as \"use the default\" rather than \"no timeout\" because a literal\n        zero would always fire and is never useful in practice.\n        \"\"\"\n        spec_timeout = spec.timeout_sec\n        if spec_timeout is not None and spec_timeout > 0:\n            return float(spec_timeout)\n        if self._default_timeout_sec is not None and self._default_timeout_sec > 0:\n            return float(self._default_timeout_sec)\n        return None\n\n    def _handle_timeout(\n        self,\n        *,\n        spec: EvalSpec,\n        log: _JsonlLog,\n        timeout_sec: Optional[float],\n    ) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Emit timeout events, attempt best-effort teardown, return outcome.\n\n        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) extends the T03.05 timeout path with a\n        subprocess kill + zombie reap step: if the wired executor\n        exposes a ``cancel()`` method (the production\n        ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` + ``PtyDriver`` does), the runner calls\n        it from the main thread so the PtyDriver child terminates and\n        ``wait()`` reaps the zombie before the orchestrator collects\n        the next outcome. Failure is swallowed because the TIMEOUT\n        outcome must always return cleanly; the JSONL log records the\n        cancel attempt.\n        \"\"\"\n        budget = float(timeout_sec) if timeout_sec is not None else 0.0\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED,\n            step=\"timeout\",\n            extra={\"timeout_sec\": budget},\n        )\n\n        # NFR-REL1: kill the executor's subprocess + reap zombie before\n        # we attempt teardown. Documented on D-0050/spec.md.\n        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"timeout\")\n\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": True},\n        )\n        try:\n            # The worker may still hold references to the HomeIsolation\n            # instance; HomeIsolation.teardown is idempotent enough that\n            # a main-thread call is safe even if the worker is mid-step.\n            # Failures are swallowed so the TIMEOUT outcome always\n            # returns cleanly.\n            if getattr(self._home, \"is_set_up\", False):\n                self._home.teardown(keep=True)\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort\n            cls = type(exc)\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n\n        outcome = EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"TIMEOUT\",\n            duration_sec=budget,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\",\n        )\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    def _cancel_executor(self, *, log: _JsonlLog, reason: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Best-effort PtyDriver kill + zombie reap on the wired executor.\n\n        The production executor (``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` driving a\n        ``PtyDriver``) exposes a ``cancel()`` method that calls\n        ``PtyDriver.terminate(force=True)`` followed by ``close()`` so\n        the child process is signalled and ``wait()`` reaps the\n        resulting zombie. Test stubs that do not need cancellation\n        simply omit the method — ``getattr`` returns ``None`` and the\n        runner skips the step.\n\n        Failures are swallowed so a flaky cancel cannot block a\n        TIMEOUT / INTERRUPTED outcome from returning. The JSONL log\n        records ``executor_cancel_*`` events under the ``timeout``\n        step so post-mortem tooling can confirm the kill attempt\n        landed.\n        \"\"\"\n        cancel = getattr(self._executor, \"cancel\", None)\n        if cancel is None or not callable(cancel):\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_SKIPPED,\n                step=reason,\n                extra={\"reason\": \"executor has no cancel() method\"},\n            )\n            return\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED,\n            step=reason,\n            extra={},\n        )\n        try:\n            cancel()\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort kill\n            cls = type(exc)\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_ERROR,\n                step=reason,\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n            return\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_COMPLETED,\n            step=reason,\n            extra={},\n        )\n\n    def _make_interrupted_outcome(\n        self, *, spec: EvalSpec, log: _JsonlLog\n    ) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome and flush the JSONL log.\n\n        Used when:\n\n        * The cancellation token was already set before\n          ``EvalRunner.run`` started (skip the lifecycle entirely).\n        * The worker raised ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` and\n          a cancellation token is wired (NFR-REL1 cooperative path).\n\n        ``duration_sec`` is pinned to ``0.0`` because no observable\n        portion of the lifecycle ran; the Reporter renders the column\n        verbatim so a stale value would confuse downstream readers.\n        \"\"\"\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_INTERRUPTED_FIRED,\n            step=\"interrupt\",\n            extra={},\n        )\n        # Best-effort cancel: if the executor was mid-flight when the\n        # interrupt arrived, kill its subprocess + reap zombie.\n        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"interrupt\")\n        # Best-effort teardown so a partial HOME (forensic artefact) is\n        # preserved when isolation already ran.\n        if getattr(self._home, \"is_set_up\", False):\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\"keep\": True},\n            )\n            try:\n                self._home.teardown(keep=True)\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort\n                cls = type(exc)\n                log.emit(\n                    EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                    step=\"teardown\",\n                    extra={\n                        \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                        \"message\": str(exc),\n                    },\n                )\n\n        outcome = EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"INTERRUPTED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    def _flush_log(self, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Write the buffered JSONL log under ``home_path/.eval-logs/``.\n\n        Best-effort: if ``home_path`` is not yet resolvable (setup\n        failed before ``home_path`` was assigned) the events are\n        discarded. The JSONL log is a forensic artifact, never\n        load-bearing for outcome classification.\n        \"\"\"\n        home_path = self._resolve_home_path()\n        if home_path is None:\n            return\n        # ``eval_id`` is regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 upstream so it is safe\n        # to use verbatim in a filename. We still take the first event\n        # for the id to avoid trusting any later mutation.\n        snapshot = log.snapshot()\n        if not snapshot:\n            return\n        eval_id = snapshot[0].eval_id\n        log_path = home_path / self.LOG_DIR_RELPATH / f\"{eval_id}.jsonl\"\n        log.write_to(log_path)\n\n    def _resolve_home_path(self) -> Optional[Path]:\n        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME path if available, ``None`` otherwise.\n\n        ``HomeIsolation.home_path`` raises when setup has not run; we\n        catch that and return ``None`` so a setup-time failure never\n        promotes itself into a runner exception.\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            return Path(self._home.home_path)\n        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — home_path may raise pre-setup\n            return None\n","numLines":1157,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1157}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C5mW4wmh3AGH3e6tZAJqub","type":"tool_result","content":"720\tThe orchestrator uses `concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor` (matching `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`'s pattern, NOT the heavier `execution/parallel.py`). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n721\t\n722\t### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n723\t\n724\t1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n725\t2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n726\t3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n727\t4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n728\t\n729\t### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n730\t\n731\tFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n732\t\n733\t### Signal handling\n734\t\n735\t- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n736\t- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n737\t- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n738\t\n739\t### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n740\t\n741\t- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n742\t- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n743\t- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n744\t\n745\t---\n746\t\n747\t## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n748\t\n749\t### What we fork\n750\t\n751\t`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` (MIT, Python 3.8+). The repo has 1 star / 0 forks — bus-factor risk is real. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n752\t\n753\t### Vendoring layout\n754\t\n755\t```\n756\tsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n757\t├── __init__.py\n758\t├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n759\t├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n760\t├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n761\t└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n762\t```\n763\t\n764\t### What we change vs upstream\n765\t\n766\t| Change | Reason |\n767\t|---|---|\n768\t| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n769\t| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n770\t| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n771\t| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n772\t| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n773\t| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n774\t\n775\t### What we DON'T change\n776\t\n777\t- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n778\t- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n779\t- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs.\n780\t\n781\t### Upstream resync policy\n782\t\n783\tThe vendored fork is \"frozen at the fork SHA.\" If upstream releases significant changes (e.g., `pexpect` major-version bump, important bugfix), the `PROVENANCE.md` documents the resync procedure: pull upstream, three-way merge our changes, re-run vendored tests.\n784\t\n785\t### License compliance\n786\t\n787\tptytest is MIT. We retain the upstream LICENSE verbatim in `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` and reference it in IronClaude's top-level NOTICE (if one exists; otherwise add one).\n788\t\n789\t---\n790\t\n791\t## 14. Risks & mitigations\n792\t\n793\t| ID | Risk | Severity | Probability | Mitigation |\n794\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n795\t| R1 | **Claude Code TTY behavior changes between versions** breaks the PtyDriver | HIGH | MEDIUM | Pin `claude` version range in `eval doctor`; capture full TTY transcripts so version-shift regressions are diagnosable. |\n796\t| R2 | **Per-eval HOME setup is slow** (deploy 9 scripts × 15 evals = 135 file ops) | MEDIUM | HIGH | Reuse `install_hooks.py` (already optimized). At ~10ms per `cp`, full setup is ~1.4s per eval. Acceptable. |\n797\t| R3 | **MCP server flakiness** causes E1/E2 false failures | MEDIUM | MEDIUM | Per-eval retry-once on MCP-specific failure modes (timeout, unreachable). Each eval runs in its own thread; the thread blocks on `PtyDriver.wait()` which is itself a `pexpect` blocking read.\n\n### Isolation guarantees at high concurrency\n\n1. **No shared mutable state** — each eval has its own `HomeIsolation` with its own HOME dir.\n2. **No shared file handles** — each eval opens its own `auggie-first.jsonl` under its HOME.\n3. **No port collisions** — Claude Code TTY doesn't bind ports for the evals we run.\n4. **Bounded process count** — at `--parallel 8`, max 8 `claude` subprocesses concurrently. At `--parallel 15`, 15. Each consumes ~150MB resident; 15-way needs ~2.25GB free.\n\n### Bounded retry (no infinite retry)\n\nFailed evals are NOT retried by default. The harness produces a deterministic single-pass run. The user can re-run with `--eval <failed-ids>` after diagnosing.\n\n### Signal handling\n\n- `SIGINT` (Ctrl-C): Cancel all running evals, mark in-flight as INTERRUPTED, write partial summary, exit 3.\n- `SIGTERM`: Same as SIGINT.\n- Per-eval timeout: kill the `PtyDriver` subprocess + reap zombie; mark eval as TIMEOUT (counts as FAIL).\n\n### What we DON'T do at high concurrency\n\n- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n\n---\n\n## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n\n### What we fork\n\n`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` (MIT, Python 3.8+). The repo has 1 star / 0 forks — bus-factor risk is real. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n\n### Vendoring layout\n\n```\nsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n├── __init__.py\n├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n```\n\n### What we change vs upstream\n\n| Change | Reason |\n|---|---|\n| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n\n### What we DON'T change\n\n- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs.\n\n### Upstream resync policy\n\nThe vendored fork is \"frozen at the fork SHA.\" If upstream releases significant changes (e.g., `pexpect` major-version bump, important bugfix), the `PROVENANCE.md` documents the resync procedure: pull upstream, three-way merge our changes, re-run vendored tests.\n\n### License compliance\n\nptytest is MIT. We retain the upstream LICENSE verbatim in `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` and reference it in IronClaude's top-level NOTICE (if one exists; otherwise add one).\n\n---\n\n## 14. Used by\n138\t      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n139\t    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. A single public entrypoint\n``run_eval(spec, ...) -> EvalOutcome`` executes the seven-step sequence:\n\n    1. build isolation  (HomeIsolation.setup)\n    2. deploy hooks     (deploy_hooks_to)\n    3. spawn            (executor.spawn)\n    4. inject           (executor.inject)\n    5. observe          (executor.observe)\n    6. assert           (each ExpectCallable applied to an EvalContext)\n    7. teardown         (HomeIsolation.teardown)\n\nand maps the outcome to a DM-001 ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01) following the\nstatus-mapping rules documented in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n* All applied ``ExpectCallable``s returned ``passed=True`` -> ``PASS``.\n* At least one ``ExpectResult.passed`` is ``False`` and no harness\n  exception fired -> ``FAIL``.\n* Any exception other than ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` raised\n  during steps 1-6 -> ``ERRORED`` with ``error_class`` = fully-qualified\n  exception classname.\n* ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` propagates so NFR-REL1\n  (T03.07) can mark the outcome ``INTERRUPTED`` at the orchestrator\n  layer.
